It reads one CSV file, infers a type for each column,\nand writes either a column profile or a frequency listing to standard output as CSV. Failures write one\ndiagnostic line to standard error and nothing to standard output.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | The whole application; `go.mod` declares no third-party requirement |\n| `go test` with `go vet` | Project test suite behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| CSV (RFC 4180, `encoding/csv`) | Input reading and output writing |\n| POSIX shell | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    module csvstat, go 1.22 or newer, no require directive\ncmd/csvstat/main.go       package main; calls cli.Run and passes the result to os.Exit\ninternal/cli              argument parsing, dispatch, exit-code mapping, diagnostics\ninternal/csvdata          CSV reading, trimming, null recognition, input rejection\ninternal/profile          type inference, counts, min/max, sum/mean/median, decimal formatting\ninternal/freq             frequency counting, ordering, limiting\nbin/test.sh               POSIX test gate (go vet ./... then go test ./...)\nsources/                  read-only scoring assets; never modified\n```\n\nEach `internal/` package carries a `doc.go` declaring its package so the layout exists before any\nbehavior is built.\n\n## Entry Contract\n\n`cli.Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int` is the single entry point. `args` excludes the\nprogram name. Only `cmd/csvstat/main.go` calls `os.Exit`; no package under `internal/` does. Tests call\n`Run` with `bytes.Buffer` writers.\n\nStandard output is written only on success: output is built in memory and flushed after every check\nhas passed.\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success |\n| 1 | Operational failure: the file cannot be opened or read |\n| 2 | Usage error: missing or extra file, unknown option, missing option value, non-positive or malformed `--top`, `--top` without `--freq`, `--column` with `--freq` |\n| 3 | Rejected input: empty file, empty or repeated header name, ragged or malformed record, or a column name absent from the header |\n\n## Package Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning package | Allowed low-level access |\n|---|---|---|\n| Command line, exit codes, stderr diagnostics | `internal/cli` | `flag`, `io`, `os.Open` for the input file |\n| CSV parsing, trimming, null spellings, rejection | `internal/csvdata` | `encoding/csv`, `io`, `strings` |\n| Type inference and statistics | `internal/profile` | `math/big`, `time`, `regexp`, `sort` |\n| Frequency counting | `internal/freq` | `sort` |\n| Configuration, file stores, external services, persistence | None; the application has no persistent state, no database, and no network access | Not applicable |\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Sums, means, and medians are computed exactly with `math/big` and rounded once, half away from zero,\n  when written.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file and never embeds a statistic,\n  a type, or a value list. Every value comes from the file examined.\n- Diagnostics go to standard error and are never compared by the grader.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-go-mod ===\nIntent: The root go.mod declares a module, Go 1.22 or newer, and no third-party requirement.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ntext = Path(\"go.mod\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\nlines = [line.strip() for line in text.splitlines()]\nmodules = [line for line in lines if line.startswith(\"module \")]\nassert len(modules) == 1, lines\nassert modules[0].split(None, 1)[1].strip() != \"\"\ngoline = [line for line in lines if re.match(r\"^go\\s+\\d+\\.\\d+\", line)]\nassert len(goline) == 1, lines\nmatch = re.match(r\"^go\\s+(\\d+)\\.(\\d+)\", goline[0])\nassert (int(match.group(1)), int(match.group(2))) >= (1, 22)\nassert not any(line.startswith(\"require\") for line in lines), lines\n=== END AC arch-go-mod ===\n\n=== AC arch-layout ===\nIntent: The application packages exist under internal/ and the command directory exists under cmd/.\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfor package in (\"cli\", \"csvdata\", \"profile\", \"freq\"):\n    directory = Path(\"internal\") / package\n    assert directory.is_dir(), directory\n    assert list(directory.glob(\"*.go\")), directory\nassert Path(\"cmd/csvstat\").is_dir()\n=== END AC arch-layout ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Do not modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute any file under `sources/`.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file and never embeds an answer.\n- Use only the Go standard library; add no third-party module.\n- Only `cmd/csvstat/main.go` calls `os.Exit`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Executable.md ===\n# FEATURE: Executable\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Build the csvstat executable from the module root with a thin main package over cli.Run. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | csvstat command, cli.Run |\n| Consumes    | None |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`go build ./cmd/csvstat` at the application root produces the `csvstat` executable using only the\nstandard library. This story delivers the entry point and a minimal `Run` that later stories extend.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `cmd/csvstat/main.go` is `package main`. It calls `cli.Run(os.Args[1:], os.Stdout, os.Stderr)` and\n  passes the returned code to `os.Exit`. It contains no other logic.\n- `internal/cli` exports `Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int`. In this story it returns 0.\n  Later stories add parsing, dispatch, and failure mapping.\n- The import path of the cli package is `<module>/internal/cli`, where the module path is the one in\n  `go.mod`.\n- Every import in the module is either standard library or module-local.\n\n## Tests For The Project Suite\n\nThe builder adds `internal/cli` tests that call `Run` with `bytes.Buffer` writers.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC exe-builds ===\nIntent: The csvstat executable builds with go build and runs to a defined exit code on a valid file.\nRequires: executable=go; `sources/`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Arguments.md ===\n# FEATURE: Arguments\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Parse csvstat options and exactly one file argument, with options preceding the file. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Executable.md |\n| Provides    | cli.ParseArgs |\n| Consumes    | cli.Run |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\ncsvstat accepts two command forms:\n\n```text\ncsvstat [--column <name>] <file>\ncsvstat --freq <name> [--top <n>] <file>\n```\n\nOptions precede the file and exactly one file is required. This story delivers the parser and its\nwiring into `Run`. Cross-option validation (`--top` without `--freq`, `--column` with `--freq`,\nnon-positive `--top`) belongs to the usage-errors story.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `internal/cli` exports `ParseArgs(args []string) (Options, error)`.\n- `Options` has fields `Column string`, `Freq string`, `Top int`, `TopSet bool`, `File string`, and\n  flags are recorded as set or not set (a presence flag for `--column` and `--freq`, so an empty value\n  is distinguishable from an absent option).\n- Parsing uses a local `flag.FlagSet` created with `flag.ContinueOnError` and its output discarded.\n  Options take a separate value (`--top 3`); a repeated option is a parse error.\n- Parsing stops at the first non-option argument. That argument is the file. Any argument after it,\n  including a later option, is an extra argument and a parse error. Options after the file are\n  therefore rejected.\n- A missing file, an extra argument, an unknown option, and an option with no value each return an\n  error. `--top` is parsed as a decimal integer; a non-integer value is a parse error.\n- `Run` calls `ParseArgs`. When it returns an error, `Run` writes one line to `stderr`, writes nothing\n  to `stdout`, and returns 2.\n\n## Tests For The Project Suite\n\nThe builder writes these `internal/cli` tests, named exactly as listed, using the named inputs:\n\n- `TestParseArgsFileOnly`: `[file]` yields File set and no options.\n- `TestParseArgsColumn`: `[--column name file]` yields Column and File.\n- `TestParseArgsFreqTop`: `[--freq name --top 3 file]` yields Freq, Top 3, TopSet, and File.\n- `TestParseArgsMissingFile`: `[--column name]` is an error.\n- `TestParseArgsExtraFile`: two files is an error.\n- `TestParseArgsUnknownOption`: `[--frobnicate file]` is an error.\n- `TestParseArgsMissingValue`: `[file-less --top]` forms with no value are an error.\n- `TestParseArgsOptionsAfterFile`: `[file --column name]` is an error.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC args-file-only ===\nIntent: A lone file argument parses.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseArgsFileOnly\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-file-only ===\n\n=== AC args-column ===\nIntent: The --column option and its value parse before the file.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseArgsColumn\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-column ===\n\n=== AC args-freq-top ===\nIntent: The --freq and --top options and their values parse before the file.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseArgsFreqTop\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-freq-top ===\n\n=== AC args-structural-errors ===\nIntent: Missing file, extra file, unknown option, missing value, and options after the file are parse errors.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nnames = [\n    \"TestParseArgsMissingFile\",\n    \"TestParseArgsExtraFile\",\n    \"TestParseArgsUnknownOption\",\n    \"TestParseArgsMissingValue\",\n    \"TestParseArgsOptionsAfterFile\",\n]\nfor name in names:\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-structural-errors ===\n\n=== AC args-valid-forms-not-usage-errors ===\nIntent: The valid command forms are accepted by the executable and never exit with the usage-error code.\nRequires: executable=go; Trimming, null recognition, and\nrejection of malformed input are separate stories that build on this reader.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `Read(r io.Reader) (*Table, error)` parses with `encoding/csv`, with `FieldsPerRecord = -1` so field\n  counts are judged by the rejection story, and `LazyQuotes` off.\n- `Table` has `Header []string` and `Records []Record`. `Record` has `Line int`, the 1-based source line\n  on which the record starts, and `Fields []string`.\n- The first record is the header; every later record is a data record. Values are returned as parsed\n  with no trimming.\n- A quoted field containing a comma is one value. A quoted field may contain doubled quotes and newlines.\n- A line ending after the last record creates no extra record. Input with or without a final newline\n  yields the same records.\n- Input with no record at all (empty or only blank lines) returns a `Table` with a nil `Header` and no\n  `Records` and no error; the input-rejection story turns that into a rejection.\n- A syntax error reported by `encoding/csv` is returned wrapped so callers can recover the line number\n  with `errors.As` and `*csv.ParseError`.\n- `Read` does not open files; `cli` opens the file and passes the reader.\n\n## Tests For The Project Suite\n\nThe builder writes these `internal/csvdata` tests, named exactly as listed:\n\n- `TestReadHeaderAndRecords`: header is the first record; the rest are data records.\n- `TestReadQuotedComma`: a quoted field with a comma is a single value.\n- `TestReadTrailingNewline`: a final newline adds no record, matching the same input without one.\n- `TestReadRecordLineNumbers`: each record carries the line on which it starts, including after a\n  quoted field that spans lines.\n- `TestReadHeaderOnly`: a header with no data yields zero records.\n- `TestReadNoRecords`: empty input yields a nil header and no records.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC read-header-and-records ===\nIntent: The first record is the header and later records are data records.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestReadHeaderAndRecords\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC read-header-and-records ===\n\n=== AC read-quoted-comma ===\nIntent: A quoted field containing a comma parses as one value.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestReadHeaderOnly\", \"TestReadNoRecords\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC read-header-only-and-empty ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Read with Go's `encoding/csv`; BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: CsvStat\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, package ownership, entry contract, and exit-code policy for the csvstat command-line CSV profiler. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | go.mod, internal/cli, internal/csvdata, internal/profile, internal/freq, cmd/csvstat |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\ncsvstat is a single Go command-line executable. It reads one CSV file, infers a type for each column,\nand writes either a column profile or a frequency listing to standard output as CSV. Failures write one\ndiagnostic line to standard error and nothing to standard output.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | The whole application; `go.mod` declares no third-party requirement |\n| `go test` with `go vet` | Project test suite behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| CSV (RFC 4180, `encoding/csv`) | Input reading and output writing |\n| POSIX shell | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    module csvstat, go 1.22 or newer, no require directive\ncmd/csvstat/main.go       package main; calls cli.Run and passes the result to os.Exit\ninternal/cli              argument parsing, dispatch, exit-code mapping, diagnostics\ninternal/csvdata          CSV reading, trimming, null recognition, input rejection\ninternal/profile          type inference, counts, min/max, sum/mean/median, decimal formatting\ninternal/freq             frequency counting, ordering, limiting\nbin/test.sh               POSIX test gate (go vet ./... then go test ./...)\nsources/                  read-only scoring assets; never modified\n```\n\nEach `internal/` package carries a `doc.go` declaring its package so the layout exists before any\nbehavior is built.\n\n## Entry Contract\n\n`cli.Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int` is the single entry point. `args` excludes the\nprogram name. Only `cmd/csvstat/main.go` calls `os.Exit`; no package under `internal/` does. Tests call\n`Run` with `bytes.Buffer` writers.\n\nStandard output is written only on success: output is built in memory and flushed after every check\nhas passed.\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success |\n| 1 | Operational failure: the file cannot be opened or read |\n| 2 | Usage error: missing or extra file, unknown option, missing option value, non-positive or malformed `--top`, `--top` without `--freq`, `--column` with `--freq` |\n| 3 | Rejected input: empty file, empty or repeated header name, ragged or malformed record, or a column name absent from the header |\n\n## Package Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning package | Allowed low-level access |\n|---|---|---|\n| Command line, exit codes, stderr diagnostics | `internal/cli` | `flag`, `io`, `os.Open` for the input file |\n| CSV parsing, trimming, null spellings, rejection | `internal/csvdata` | `encoding/csv`, `io`, `strings` |\n| Type inference and statistics | `internal/profile` | `math/big`, `time`, `regexp`, `sort` |\n| Frequency counting | `internal/freq` | `sort` |\n| Configuration, file stores, external services, persistence | None; the application has no persistent state, no database, and no network access | Not applicable |\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Sums, means, and medians are computed exactly with `math/big` and rounded once, half away from zero,\n  when written.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file and never embeds a statistic,\n  a type, or a value list. Every value comes from the file examined.\n- Diagnostics go to standard error and are never compared by the grader.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-go-mod ===\nIntent: The root go.mod declares a module, Go 1.22 or newer, and no third-party requirement.\nRequires: executable=go; exactly one file is required. This story delivers the parser and its\nwiring into `Run`. Cross-option validation (`--top` without `--freq`, `--column` with `--freq`,\nnon-positive `--top`) belongs to the usage-errors story.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `internal/cli` exports `ParseArgs(args []string) (Options, error)`.\n- `Options` has fields `Column string`, `Freq string`, `Top int`, `TopSet bool`, `File string`, and\n  flags are recorded as set or not set (a presence flag for `--column` and `--freq`, so an empty value\n  is distinguishable from an absent option).\n- Parsing uses a local `flag.FlagSet` created with `flag.ContinueOnError` and its output discarded.\n  Options take a separate value (`--top 3`); a repeated option is a parse error.\n- Parsing stops at the first non-option argument. That argument is the file. Any argument after it,\n  including a later option, is an extra argument and a parse error. Options after the file are\n  therefore rejected.\n- A missing file, an extra argument, an unknown option, and an option with no value each return an\n  error. `--top` is parsed as a decimal integer; a non-integer value is a parse error.\n- `Run` calls `ParseArgs`. When it returns an error, `Run` writes one line to `stderr`, writes nothing\n  to `stdout`, and returns 2.\n\n## Tests For The Project Suite\n\nThe builder writes these `internal/cli` tests, named exactly as listed, using the named inputs:\n\n- `TestParseArgsFileOnly`: `[file]` yields File set and no options.\n- `TestParseArgsColumn`: `[--column name file]` yields Column and File.\n- `TestParseArgsFreqTop`: `[--freq name --top 3 file]` yields Freq, Top 3, TopSet, and File.\n- `TestParseArgsMissingFile`: `[--column name]` is an error.\n- `TestParseArgsExtraFile`: two files is an error.\n- `TestParseArgsUnknownOption`: `[--frobnicate file]` is an error.\n- `TestParseArgsMissingValue`: `[file-less --top]` forms with no value are an error.\n- `TestParseArgsOptionsAfterFile`: `[file --column name]` is an error.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC args-file-only ===\nIntent: A lone file argument parses.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseArgsFileOnly\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-file-only ===\n\n=== AC args-column ===\nIntent: The --column option and its value parse before the file.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseArgsColumn\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-column ===\n\n=== AC args-freq-top ===\nIntent: The --freq and --top options and their values parse before the file.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseArgsFreqTop\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-freq-top ===\n\n=== AC args-structural-errors ===\nIntent: Missing file, extra file, unknown option, missing value, and options after the file are parse errors.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nnames = [\n    \"TestParseArgsMissingFile\",\n    \"TestParseArgsExtraFile\",\n    \"TestParseArgsUnknownOption\",\n    \"TestParseArgsMissingValue\",\n    \"TestParseArgsOptionsAfterFile\",\n]\nfor name in names:\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC args-structural-errors ===\n\n=== AC args-valid-forms-not-usage-errors ===\nIntent: The valid command forms are accepted by the executable and never exit with the usage-error code.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport csv\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nusage_exit = 2\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    path = os.path.join(workdir, \"data.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", newline=\"\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n        writer = csv.writer(handle, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\n        writer.writerow([\"a\", \"b\"])\n        writer.writerow([\"1\", \"2\"])\n    forms = [\n        [path],\n        [\"--column\", \"a\", path],\n        [\"--freq\", \"a\", path],\n        [\"--freq\", \"a\", \"--top\", \"3\", path],\n    ]\n    for form in forms:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(*form)\n        print(form, code, out, err)\n        assert code != usage_exit, form\n=== END AC args-valid-forms-not-usage-errors ===\n\n=== AC args-options-after-file-exit ===\nIntent: An option placed after the file is rejected by the executable with the usage-error exit status and no standard output.\n\nimport csv\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    path = os.path.join(workdir, \"data.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", newline=\"\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n        writer = csv.writer(handle, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\n        writer.writerow([\"a\", \"b\"])\n        writer.writerow([\"1\", \"2\"])\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(path, \"--column\", \"a\")\n    print(code, out, err)\n    assert code == 2\n    assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC args-options-after-file-exit ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Options precede the file; Trimming, null recognition, and\nrejection of malformed input are separate stories that build on this reader.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `Read(r io.Reader) (*Table, error)` parses with `encoding/csv`, with `FieldsPerRecord = -1` so field\n  counts are judged by the rejection story, and `LazyQuotes` off.\n- `Table` has `Header []string` and `Records []Record`. `Record` has `Line int`, the 1-based source line\n  on which the record starts, and `Fields []string`.\n- The first record is the header; every later record is a data record. Values are returned as parsed\n  with no trimming.\n- A quoted field containing a comma is one value. A quoted field may contain doubled quotes and newlines.\n- A line ending after the last record creates no extra record. Input with or without a final newline\n  yields the same records.\n- Input with no record at all (empty or only blank lines) returns a `Table` with a nil `Header` and no\n  `Records` and no error; the input-rejection story turns that into a rejection.\n- A syntax error reported by `encoding/csv` is returned wrapped so callers can recover the line number\n  with `errors.As` and `*csv.ParseError`.\n- `Read` does not open files; `cli` opens the file and passes the reader.\n\n## Tests For The Project Suite\n\nThe builder writes these `internal/csvdata` tests, named exactly as listed:\n\n- `TestReadHeaderAndRecords`: header is the first record; the rest are data records.\n- `TestReadQuotedComma`: a quoted field with a comma is a single value.\n- `TestReadTrailingNewline`: a final newline adds no record, matching the same input without one.\n- `TestReadRecordLineNumbers`: each record carries the line on which it starts, including after a\n  quoted field that spans lines.\n- `TestReadHeaderOnly`: a header with no data yields zero records.\n- `TestReadNoRecords`: empty input yields a nil header and no records.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC read-header-and-records ===\nIntent: The first record is the header and later records are data records.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestReadHeaderAndRecords\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC read-header-and-records ===\n\n=== AC read-quoted-comma ===\nIntent: A quoted field containing a comma parses as one value.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestReadQuotedComma\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC read-quoted-comma ===\n\n=== AC read-trailing-newline ===\nIntent: A line ending after the last record does not create an extra data record.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestReadTrailingNewline\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC read-trailing-newline ===\n\n=== AC read-line-numbers ===\nIntent: Each record carries the source line on which it starts.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestReadRecordLineNumbers\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC read-line-numbers ===\n\n=== AC read-header-only-and-empty ===\nIntent: A header-only input has zero records and an input with no record has no header.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestReadHeaderOnly\", \"TestReadNoRecords\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC read-header-only-and-empty ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Read with Go's `encoding/csv`; do not write a custom CSV parser.\n- The reader does not trim, classify, or reject values; those belong to later stories.\n- Never read a supplied data file from application code; the file named on the command line is the only input.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Trimming.md ===\n# FEATURE: Trimming\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Trim leading and trailing spaces from every header name and every value before any other rule applies. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CsvReading.md |\n| Provides    | csvdata.Trim |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.Read |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery header name and every value is trimmed of leading and trailing spaces before null recognition,\ntyping, counting, or comparison. Padded text such as `  Globex  ` and `Acme Corp ` therefore count as\n`Globex` and `Acme Corp`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `Trim(t *Table)` replaces, in place, each header name and each record field with the same string\n  stripped of leading and trailing space characters (U+0020).\n- Only U+0020 is trimmed. Tabs and other whitespace are preserved. Interior spaces are preserved.\n- Trimming applies after CSV parsing, so spaces inside a quoted field are also trimmed.\n- Record line numbers and field counts are unchanged.\n- The cli pipeline calls `Read` then `Trim` before any other rule; the profile-output and\n  frequency-mode stories wire that call.\n\n## Tests For The Project Suite\n\nThe builder writes these `internal/csvdata` tests, named exactly as listed:\n\n- `TestTrimHeaderNames`: padded header names are trimmed.\n- `TestTrimValues`: padded values are trimmed on both sides.\n- `TestTrimQuotedPadding`: spaces inside a quoted field are trimmed, and the comma inside the quotes is kept.\n- `TestTrimOnlySpaces`: tabs are preserved and interior spaces are kept.\n- `TestTrimKeepsLines`: line numbers and field counts are unchanged.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC trim-header-names ===\nIntent: Leading and trailing spaces are removed from header names.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestTrimHeaderNames\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC trim-header-names ===\n\n=== AC trim-values ===\nIntent: Leading and trailing spaces are removed from every value.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestTrimValues\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC trim-values ===\n\n=== AC trim-quoted-padding ===\nIntent: Padding inside a quoted field is trimmed while the quoted comma is kept.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestTrimQuotedPadding\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC trim-quoted-padding ===\n\n=== AC trim-scope ===\nIntent: Only spaces are trimmed, interior spaces remain, and line numbers and field counts are unchanged.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport sys\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestTrimOnlySpaces\", \"TestTrimKeepsLines\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC trim-scope ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Trimming happens before null recognition, typing, counting, and comparison.\n- Trim leading and trailing spaces only;
